```json
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Scale-dependent attenuation.}\nAt 14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$: signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with the tail-narrowing theory of \\citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.\nReference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the drop (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may recover part of the lost signal; under 25--50\\% accumulation at 1--1.5B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells despite 8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation."
```

```json
    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Scale-dependent attenuation.}\nAt 14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$: signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with the tail-narrowing theory of \\citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.\nCross-reference control shows reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may recover part of the lost signal.\nUnder 25--50\\% accumulation, accuracy drops 8--15\\,pp yet $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 tested cells, indicating depth boundaries remain distinguishable in realistic mixing.\nDepth estimation complements binary detection: binary detectors handle the human-vs-machine boundary ($>$97\\% d0 recall) while depth features provide finer ordinal resolution that binary methods cannot.",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Scale-dependent attenuation.}\nAt 14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$: signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with the tail-narrowing theory of \\citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.\nReference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the drop (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may recover part of the lost signal; under 25--50\\% accumulation at 1--1.5B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells despite 8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent attenuation.}\nAt 14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$: signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with the tail-narrowing theory of \\citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.\nCross-reference control shows reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may recover part of the lost signal.\nUnder 25--50\\% accumulation, accuracy drops 8--15\\,pp yet $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 tested cells, indicating depth boundaries remain distinguishable in realistic mixing.\nDepth estimation complements binary detection: binary detectors handle the human-vs-machine boundary ($>$97\\% d0 recall) while depth features provide finer ordinal resolution that binary methods cannot.\n",
```

```json
  "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.\nAt 14B, signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), and under 25--50\\% accumulation at 1--1.5B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells despite 8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation.\n"
}
```

```json
    "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.\nAt 14B, signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), and under 25--50\\% accumulation at 1--1.5B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells despite 8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent attenuation.}\nAt 14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$: signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with the tail-narrowing theory of \\citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.\nReference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the drop (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may recover part of the lost signal; under 25--50\\% accumulation at 1--1.5B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells despite 8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation.\n",
```

```json
    "newString": "At 14B, signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may partially recover the lost signal.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.\nAt 14B, signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), and under 25--50\\% accumulation at 1--1.5B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells despite 8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation.\n",
```

```json
    "oldString": "At 14B, signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may partially recover the lost signal.",
    "newString": "Scale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) is consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch (${\\sim}$7\\,pp) suggests scale-matched references may partially recover the lost signal.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.\nAt 14B, signal exists ($2{\\times}$ chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination does not survive the scale transition, consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch accounts for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), suggesting scale-matched references may partially recover the lost signal.\n",

          " A pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.",
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - The PI is finalizing a paper "Recursive Generation Depth Estimation via Statistical Fingerprints" for EMNLP 2026 ARR (DDL May 25, 2026, today is May 22). Strict 8-page main text limit; Limitations/Ethics don't count.
   - User said "你不要管提交 ARR 的事，我会手动去处理的" (don't handle ARR submission).
   - Em dashes (—) are forbidden in paper text (user hard constraint).
   - Key users: 冯元宁 (primary), 周俊伟, 曹晓玉.
   - Current phase: final paper polishing + Discussion expansion + running plan_018 (Binoculars ordinal baseline) on GPU.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **EDD (Effective Discriminable Depth)**: Contiguous discriminable prefix from d0; binary RF with 500 trees, 5-fold CV. EDD_0.70=3 preserved in 5/6 accumulation cells.
   - **Accumulation vs Full-replacement**: Full-replacement = each generation replaces prior training set. Accumulation = synthetic + human data co-exist at 25%/50% mixing ratios.
   - **CPI (Conditional Permutation Importance)**: Addresses VIF>10 collinearity. Top-2: var_surprisal (rank 1.4) and p90_ppl (rank 1.7).
   - **Two-stage pipeline**: Strategy classifier (57.8%) → conditioned depth classifier. Pipeline 70.5% vs LOO 59.1% (+11.5pp). Oracle routing 78.3%.
   - **Multi-channel depth encoding**: 4 importance measures (Kendall's W=0.90) + Binoculars independent pathway (78.1% vs 78.4%, merged 82.6%).
   - **Scale-dependent attenuation**: At 14B, EDD_0.70=0, signal exists (2× chance) but adjacent-depth discrimination fails. Reference mismatch ~7pp.
   - **Wild-data FPR**: C4=34%, FineWeb=17.2%, RedPajama=15.5% base; thresholding T=0.10 reduces to 0.1-7%.
   - **Bootstrap CIs**: B=10000 bootstrap for Table 1, all 9 cells within 0.2pp of paper values. CI half-widths 0.46-0.59.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`docs/paper/conclusion.tex`** (Limitations section)
     - Updated wild-data FPR from "22%" to "15-34% base rate across C4, FineWeb, and RedPajama" with thresholding
     - Added specific failure cell detail: "GPT-2 XL nucleus at 50% drops to EDD_0.70=2; d2v3 AUC=0.695, marginally below threshold; Table~\ref{tab:accum_auc}"
     - Current state (lines 4-9):
     ```latex
     \paragraph{Full-replacement vs.\ accumulation.}
     The primary benchmark uses full-replacement chains; real-world contamination involves accumulation at unknown mixing ratios \cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate}.
     At 25--50\% synthetic mixing, accuracy drops 8--15\,pp and $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 tested cells (GPT-2~XL nucleus at 50\% drops to $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$; d2v3 AUC\,=\,0.695, marginally below threshold; Table~\ref{tab:accum_auc}); extension to larger models and higher ratios remains future work.
     
     \paragraph{Scale and generalization boundaries.}
     At 14B, cross-reference accuracy drops to 44.1\% ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$), with reference-model mismatch accounting for ${\sim}$7\,pp; cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\%) and web-crawl false positive rates (15--34\% base rate across C4, FineWeb, and RedPajama) are partially resolved by confidence thresholding ($T{=}0.10$ reduces FPR to 0.1--7\%) but not fully eliminated.
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/section_appendix.tex`**
     - Added new subsection "Accumulation Pairwise AUC" (after Cross-Strategy Transfer, before DCOL):
     ```latex
     \subsection{Accumulation Pairwise AUC}
     \label{app:accumulation_auc}
     
     Table~\ref{tab:accum_auc} reports pairwise AUC for the six accumulation cells (3 models $\times$ 2 mixing ratios, all nucleus).
     At 25\% mixing, all three cells preserve $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$.
     At 50\% mixing, GPT-2~XL nucleus is the sole failure: d2v3 AUC\,=\,0.695, marginally below the 0.70 threshold (0.55\,pp).
     
     \begin{table}[h]
     \centering
     \small
     \caption{Pairwise AUC under accumulation (nucleus, binary RF, 5-fold CV). Bold: $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ boundary failure.}
     \label{tab:accum_auc}

     \toprule
     ```

     ```latex
     \section{Discussion and Conclusion}
     \label{sec:discussion}
     
     \paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}
     Four importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\% vs.\ 78.4\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\%).
     Recursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.
     
     \paragraph{Deployment considerations.}
     The strategy effect (9.3\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.
     A pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.
     Scale attenuation at 14B (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) is consistent with tail-narrowing theory \citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch (${\sim}$7\,pp) suggests scale-matched references may partially recover the lost signal.
     ```

     ```latex
     Cross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\% (four-class: 59.1\%), 7\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).
     A two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\to$ conditioned depth classifier) recovers much of this gap: 70.5\% four-class accuracy ($+$11.5\,pp over LOO); oracle routing yields 78.3\%, so the 7.8\,pp residual is attributable to strategy misidentification (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
     Our depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.
     ```

```json
        "content": "\\section{Generalization and Robustness}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).\n\n\\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}\n\\label{sec:gen:baselines}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.\nFour classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.\n\n\\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA) underperforms RF by 16--32\\,pp (d2 recall 8.4\\%); RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive; 64.5\\%) narrows the gap but remains 14\\,pp below RF.\n\n\\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}\nPerturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\\% Prob, 33.2\\%; Min-K\\%++ \\cite{zhang2024_minkpp} comparable) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}); CORAL calibration does not close the gap (d1--d3: 40.4\\% vs.\\ RF 71.6\\%).\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024}, calibrated with 11-feature RF, independently converges to 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ our 78.4\\%); merging all 30 features yields 82.6\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:feature_ablation_bino}).\n\n\\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}\nSingle-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\\%); zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) also fail; depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\\% / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). $^\\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Calibration & 4-Cl (\\%) & d1--d3 (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\nBinoculars$^\\dagger$ \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\\\\nMerged (30 feat)$^\\dagger$ & RF & \\textbf{82.6} & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\\\\nRoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob \\cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT \\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\\%; QWK\\,=\\,0.81; 94.8\\% errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\\%, $2.7{\\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ conditioned depth classifier) recovers much of this gap: 70.5\\% four-class accuracy ($+$11.5\\,pp over LOO); oracle routing yields 78.3\\%, so the 7.8\\,pp residual is attributable to strategy misidentification (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).\nCross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.",
```

```json
        "content": "\\section*{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Full-replacement vs.\\ accumulation.}\nThe primary benchmark uses full-replacement chains; real-world contamination involves accumulation at unknown mixing ratios \\cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate}.\nAt 25--50\\% synthetic mixing, accuracy drops 8--15\\,pp and $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 tested cells (GPT-2~XL nucleus at 50\\% drops to $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$; d2v3 AUC\\,=\\,0.695, marginally below threshold; Table~\\ref{tab:accum_auc}); extension to larger models and higher ratios remains future work.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale and generalization boundaries.}\nAt 14B, cross-reference accuracy drops to 44.1\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$), with reference-model mismatch accounting for ${\\sim}$7\\,pp; cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\\%) and web-crawl false positive rates (15--34\\% base rate across C4, FineWeb, and RedPajama) are partially resolved by confidence thresholding ($T{=}0.10$ reduces FPR to 0.1--7\\%) but not fully eliminated.\n\n\\paragraph{Reference model dependency.}\nThe pipeline requires a fixed reference model; cross-architecture references degrade accuracy by 4--11\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:ref_ablation}), and no principled selection criterion exists beyond empirical ablation.\nAdditional caveats (single LoRA seed with ${\\sim}$1--3\\,pp uncertainty, adversarial robustness, passage length sensitivity) are detailed in the Appendix.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work uses publicly available pretrained language models (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, GPT-2~XL) and Wikipedia text as seed data.\nNo human subjects were involved, no private or sensitive data were collected, and no new models were released.\nOur method is intended to support data provenance and training-data auditing; we acknowledge that depth estimation tools could in principle be misused to circumvent content moderation, though such risks are shared with all detection-oriented research.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.\nAI assistants were used in an auxiliary role for coding support and language polishing.\nAll scientific decisions, experimental design, analysis, and core writing were conducted by the authors.",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 22
      }
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}
5	Four importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\% vs.\ 78.4\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\%).
6	Recursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.
7	
8	\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}
9	The strategy effect (9.3\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.
10	A pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.
11	Scale attenuation at 14B (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) is consistent with tail-narrowing theory \citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch (${\sim}$7\,pp) suggests scale-matched references may partially recover the lost signal.
12	
```

```text
72	\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}
73	\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}
74	
75	Cross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\% (four-class: 59.1\%), 7\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).
76	A two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\to$ conditioned depth classifier) recovers much of this gap: 70.5\% four-class accuracy ($+$11.5\,pp over LOO); oracle routing yields 78.3\%, so the 7.8\,pp residual is attributable to strategy misidentification (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
77	Our depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.
78	
79	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
80	\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}
81	\label{sec:gen:crossscale}
82	
83	At 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\% ($2{\times}$ chance, $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\% ($-$6.8\,pp) but raises $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\sim}$7\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).
84	Cross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.
```

```json
      "content": "\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ conditioned depth classifier) recovers much of this gap: 70.5\\% four-class accuracy ($+$11.5\\,pp over LOO); oracle routing yields 78.3\\%, so the 7.8\\,pp residual is attributable to strategy misidentification (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).\nCross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA pilot curation experiment (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) shows RF-based depth filtering retains 44\\% of the corpus while matching unfiltered perplexity and outperforming random filtering ($p{<}0.002$); the practical path is depth-scored data auditing rather than direct training-set filtering.\nScale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) is consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; reference-model mismatch (${\\sim}$7\\,pp) suggests scale-matched references may partially recover the lost signal.\n",
```

```text
60	
61	\paragraph{Setup.}
62	CPI is computed on all 9 model$\times$strategy cells.
63	For each cell, 1{,}000 bootstrap resamples produce a rank distribution per feature; the 95\% bootstrap CI width quantifies rank stability (width $\leq 1.0$ indicates near-invariant rankings across resamples).
64	
65	\paragraph{Results.}
66	Table~\ref{tab:conditional_pi} summarizes the top-5 features by mean CPI across cells.
67	\texttt{var\_surprisal} (avg rank 1.4, CPI\,=\,0.260) and \texttt{p90\_ppl} (avg rank 1.7, CPI\,=\,0.238) are the top-2 in 8/9 cells, with bootstrap rank CI widths of 0.50 and 0.30---both $\leq 0.5$, indicating near-invariant rankings across resamples.
68	Ranks 3--5 are \texttt{p75\_ppl}, \texttt{p50\_ppl}, and a cell-dependent set (\texttt{p25\_ppl}, \texttt{mean\_ppl}, or \texttt{trigram\_entropy}), all with CI width $< 0.5$.
69	
70	\begin{table}[h]
71	\centering
72	\small
73	\caption{Conditional permutation importance aggregated across 9 model$\times$strategy cells (1{,}000 bootstrap resamples). Avg Rank and Avg CPI: means over cells. Rank CI Width: mean 95\% bootstrap CI width for the rank. Bottom rows: feature-family averages.}
74	\label{tab:conditional_pi}
75	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
76	\toprule
77	Feature / Family & Avg Rank & Avg CPI & Rank CI Width \\
78	\midrule
79	\texttt{var\_surprisal} & 1.4 & 0.260 & 0.50 \\
80	\texttt{p90\_ppl} & 1.7 & 0.238 & 0.30 \\
81	\texttt{p75\_ppl} & 3 & --- & $< 0.5$ \\
82	\texttt{p50\_ppl} & 4 & --- & $< 0.5$ \\
83	\texttt{p25\_ppl / mean\_ppl}$^\dagger$ & 5 & --- & $< 0.5$ \\
84	\midrule
85	Surprisal family & 7.3 & 0.089 & --- \\
86	Perplexity family & 7.5 & 0.039 & --- \\
87	Lexical family & 9.7 & 0.004 & --- \\
88	\bottomrule
89	\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\footnotesize $^\dagger$Rank-5 varies by cell; \texttt{trigram\_entropy} appears in 1 cell.}
90	\end{tabular}
91	\end{table}
92	
93	Two exceptions exist among GPT-2~XL cells: in temperature sampling, \texttt{p90\_ppl} (CPI\,=\,0.419) exceeds \texttt{var\_surprisal} (0.093) while both remain in the top-2; in top-$k$ sampling, \texttt{p90\_ppl} (0.475) and \texttt{p75\_ppl} (0.127) push \texttt{var\_surprisal} to rank~3 (0.037), the only cell where it exits the top-2.
94	Both reversals are consistent with the Gini rankings for these cells (Table~\ref{tab:feature_importance}).
95	
96	At the family level, surprisal (avg CPI\,=\,0.089, rank\,=\,7.3) and perplexity (0.039, rank\,=\,7.5) are closely matched, while lexical features (0.004, rank\,=\,9.7) contribute minimally---consistent with the group ablation in \S\ref{sec:atlas:features}.
97	
98	\paragraph{Consistency with Gini rankings.}
99	CPI and Gini rankings agree on the dominant feature (\texttt{var\_surprisal} or \texttt{p90\_ppl}) in 8/9 cells; the sole disagreement (GPT-2~XL top-$k$) involves a swap between rank-2 and rank-3 features.
100	Bootstrap rank CI widths $\leq 0.5$ for the top-2 features confirm that these rankings are not artifacts of collinearity or resampling variability.
101	Controlling for multicollinearity via conditional permutation corroborates the Gini- and SHAP-based importance hierarchy reported in the main text.

103	\subsection{Feature Trajectories}
```

```json
      "content": "\n\\paragraph{Setup.}\nCPI is computed on all 9 model$\\times$strategy cells.\nFor each cell, 1{,}000 bootstrap resamples produce a rank distribution per feature; the 95\\% bootstrap CI width quantifies rank stability (width $\\leq 1.0$ indicates near-invariant rankings across resamples).\n\n\\paragraph{Results.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:conditional_pi} summarizes the top-5 features by mean CPI across cells.\n\\texttt{var\\_surprisal} (avg rank 1.4, CPI\\,=\\,0.260) and \\texttt{p90\\_ppl} (avg rank 1.7, CPI\\,=\\,0.238) are the top-2 in 8/9 cells, with bootstrap rank CI widths of 0.50 and 0.30---both $\\leq 0.5$, indicating near-invariant rankings across resamples.\nRanks 3--5 are \\texttt{p75\\_ppl}, \\texttt{p50\\_ppl}, and a cell-dependent set (\\texttt{p25\\_ppl}, \\texttt{mean\\_ppl}, or \\texttt{trigram\\_entropy}), all with CI width $< 0.5$.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Conditional permutation importance aggregated across 9 model$\\times$strategy cells (1{,}000 bootstrap resamples). Avg Rank and Avg CPI: means over cells. Rank CI Width: mean 95\\% bootstrap CI width for the rank. Bottom rows: feature-family averages.}\n\\label{tab:conditional_pi}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nFeature / Family & Avg Rank & Avg CPI & Rank CI Width \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{var\\_surprisal} & 1.4 & 0.260 & 0.50 \\\\\n\\texttt{p90\\_ppl} & 1.7 & 0.238 & 0.30 \\\\\n\\texttt{p75\\_ppl} & 3 & --- & $< 0.5$ \\\\\n\\texttt{p50\\_ppl} & 4 & --- & $< 0.5$ \\\\\n\\texttt{p25\\_ppl / mean\\_ppl}$^\\dagger$ & 5 & --- & $< 0.5$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nSurprisal family & 7.3 & 0.089 & --- \\\\\nPerplexity family & 7.5 & 0.039 & --- \\\\\nLexical family & 9.7 & 0.004 & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$Rank-5 varies by cell; \\texttt{trigram\\_entropy} appears in 1 cell.}\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nTwo exceptions exist among GPT-2~XL cells: in temperature sampling, \\texttt{p90\\_ppl} (CPI\\,=\\,0.419) exceeds \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} (0.093) while both remain in the top-2; in top-$k$ sampling, \\texttt{p90\\_ppl} (0.475) and \\texttt{p75\\_ppl} (0.127) push \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} to rank~3 (0.037), the only cell where it exits the top-2.\nBoth reversals are consistent with the Gini rankings for these cells (Table~\\ref{tab:feature_importance}).\n\nAt the family level, surprisal (avg CPI\\,=\\,0.089, rank\\,=\\,7.3) and perplexity (0.039, rank\\,=\\,7.5) are closely matched, while lexical features (0.004, rank\\,=\\,9.7) contribute minimally---consistent with the group ablation in \\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}.\n\n\\paragraph{Consistency with Gini rankings.}\nCPI and Gini rankings agree on the dominant feature (\\texttt{var\\_surprisal} or \\texttt{p90\\_ppl}) in 8/9 cells; the sole disagreement (GPT-2~XL top-$k$) involves a swap between rank-2 and rank-3 features.\nBootstrap rank CI widths $\\leq 0.5$ for the top-2 features confirm that these rankings are not artifacts of collinearity or resampling variability.\nControlling for multicollinearity via conditional permutation corroborates the Gini- and SHAP-based importance hierarchy reported in the main text.\n\n\\subsection{Feature Trajectories}\n\\label{app:feature_trajectories}\n\n\\begin{figure}[h]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_feature_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{Feature trajectories across depths $d{=}0$ to $d{=}3$, stratified by model (columns: Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, GPT-2~XL) and strategy (line style). <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>
```

```text
I can see Table 7 on page 14. It looks like the table itself is fine - it's a small table with 4 columns. But let me look more carefully at the right side... Actually, I also see Table 8 (Per-Class Recall) and Table 9 on this page, plus the confusion matrix figure. Let me look more carefully.

Wait, the user said "Table7怎么有和右边overlap了" - Table 7 overlaps with the right side. Looking at the image, Table 7 is the CPI table at the top of page 14. It seems to be fine from my rendering... 

But maybe at higher resolution I can see the overlap. Looking at the rendered page 14 more carefully, I can see Table 7 at the top, followed by text about consistency with Gini rankings, then Table 8 on the right column, and Figure 5 with the confusion matrices at the bottom. The compile log actually flagged an overfull hbox warning about 40 points of overflow in the table content around lines 75-91 of section_appendix.tex, which could be causing the layout issue the user is seeing.

The problematic table is likely the `tab:conditional_pi` table that starts at line 75, though the overfull hbox might be coming from the footnote in the multicolumn rather than the table structure itself, even though it uses the compact `@{}lccc@{}` format.

Since this is an EMNLP paper using the two-column ACL format, the table is constrained to fit within a single column width. The issue is probably that the feature names in the first column—like `\texttt{var\_surprisal}` and `\texttt{p25\_ppl / mean\_ppl}$^\dagger$`—are too wide for the available space, causing the content to overflow horizontally.

The most straightforward solution is to wrap the entire tabular environment with `\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{...}` to automatically scale the table down to fit the column width.
```
